Implement the following plan:

# Plan: Rename `config_key_value` → `config` with nested table support

## Context

`[cargo.config_key_value]` uses quoted dotted keys which are hard to read:
```toml
[cargo.config_key_value]
"profile.release.opt-level" = "z"
"profile.release.codegen-units" = 1
```

Renaming to `config` and supporting nested TOML tables enables the ergonomic form:
```toml
[cargo.config.profile.release]
opt-level = "z"
codegen-units = 1
```

Both flat and nested styles will work — TOML treats them equivalently.
This is a pure passthrough to `cargo --config KEY=VALUE`.

## Changes

### 1. `src/types.rs` — Rename field, add `Table` variant to `ConfigValue`

- Add `Table(BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>)` variant to `ConfigValue`
- Rename `config_key_value` → `config` in `CargoConfig`
- Add `Display` impl for the new `Table` variant (not expected in output, but needed for completeness)
- Add `flatten_config()` function that recursively walks the nested `BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>` and produces `Vec<(String, String)>` of dotted-key/value pairs for `--config`

## Key implementation detail: `flatten_config()`

```rust
/// Flatten nested config into dotted key-value pairs for --config args.
pub fn flatten_config(config: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    let mut result = Vec::new();
    flatten_inner(config, String::new(), &mut result);
    result
}

fn flatten_inner(
    map: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>,
    prefix: String,
    result: &mut Vec<(String, String)>,
) {
    for (key, value) in map {
        let full_key = if prefix.is_empty() {
            key.clone()
        } else {
            format!("{}.{}", prefix, key)
        };
        match value {
            ConfigValue::Table(inner) => flatten_inner(inner, full_key, result),
            other => result.push((full_key, other.to_string())),
        }
    }
}
```

    11→use crate::tspec::{expand_target_dir, load_spec, spec_name_from_path};

    13→
    14→const TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER: &str = "// Generated by tspec - do not edit";
    15→pub fn warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale: bool) {
    16→    if had_stale {
    17→        println!("Warning: a stale build.rs was deleted, likely from a previous interrupted build");
    18→    }
    19→}
    20→
    21→/// Check if a build.rs file was generated by tspec.
    22→/// Matches either the marker comment or a file that only contains
    23→/// `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=` println lines (marker-less variant).
    24→fn is_tspec_generated_build_rs(content: &str) -> bool {
    25→    if content.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER) {
    26→        return true;
    27→    }
    28→    // Fallback: file only contains fn main, cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin printlns, and braces
    29→    let dominated_by_link_args = content
    30→        .lines()
    31→        .map(|l| l.trim())
    32→        .filter(|l| !l.is_empty())
    33→        .all(|l| {
    34→            l == "fn main() {"
    35→                || l == "}"
    36→                || l.starts_with(TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER)
    37→                || l.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
    38→        });
    39→    dominated_by_link_args && content.contains("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=")
    40→}
    41→
    42→/// Remove a stale tspec-generated build.rs left behind by an interrupted build.
    43→/// Returns true if a stale file was removed. Caller should print a warning after the build.
    44→pub fn remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(pkg_dir: &Path) -> bool {
    45→    let build_rs = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
    46→    if let Ok(content) = fs::read_to_string(&build_rs)
    47→        && is_tspec_generated_build_rs(&content)
    48→    {
    49→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs);
    50→        return true;
    51→    }
    52→    false
    53→}
    54→
    55→/// Result of a successful build
    56→pub struct BuildResult {
    57→    pub binary_path: PathBuf,
    58→    /// The effective target directory base (e.g., "target/foo" if target_dir was set)
    59→    pub target_base: PathBuf,
    60→}
    61→
    62→/// Build a package with a spec, returns the binary path on success
    63→pub fn build_package(pkg_name: &str, tspec: Option<&str>, release: bool) -> Result<BuildResult> {
    64→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
    65→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
    66→    let tspec_path = find_tspec(&pkg_dir, tspec)?;
    67→
    68→    // Resolve actual package name from Cargo.toml (needed when pkg_name is a path)
    69→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
    70→
    71→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
    72→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
    73→
    74→    // Track if we generated a build.rs
    75→    let build_rs_path = pkg_dir.join("build.rs");
    76→    let had_build_rs = build_rs_path.exists();
    77→
    78→    // Load spec once (if present) and compute target_dir
    79→    let (spec, expanded_td) = if let Some(path) = &tspec_path {
    80→        let s = load_spec(path)?;
    81→        let name = spec_name_from_path(path);
    82→        let td = expand_target_dir(&s, &name)?;
    83→        (Some(s), td)
    84→    } else {
    85→        (None, None)
    86→    };
    87→
    88→    // Compute paths
    89→    let target_base = match &expanded_td {
    90→        Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
    91→        None => workspace.join("target"),
    92→    };
    93→    let binary_path = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
    94→        get_binary_path(&workspace, &pkg_name, spec, release, expanded_td.as_deref())
    95→    } else {
    96→        get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, release)
    97→    };
    98→
    99→    // Apply spec if present, otherwise plain cargo build
   100→    let status = if let Some(spec) = &spec {
   101→        let path = tspec_path.as_ref().unwrap();
   102→        println!("Building {} with spec {}", pkg_name, path.display());
   103→
   104→        // Generate temporary build.rs for linker flags if needed
   105→        let has_linker_args = !spec.linker.args.is_empty();
   106→        let has_bin_target = pkg_dir.join("src/main.rs").exists();
   107→        if has_linker_args && !has_bin_target {
   108→            println!(
   109→                "Warning: linker.args ignored for {} (no binary target)",
   110→                pkg_name
   111→            );
   112→        }
   113→        if has_linker_args && has_bin_target && !had_build_rs {
   114→            generate_build_rs(&build_rs_path, &pkg_name, spec)?;
   115→        }
   116→
   117→        let mut cmd = build_cargo_command(spec)?;
   118→        cmd.arg("build");
   119→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   120→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   121→
   122→        // Set spec path for tspec-build library to read in build.rs
   123→        cmd.env("TSPEC_SPEC_FILE", path.as_os_str());
   124→
   125→        apply_spec_to_command(&mut cmd, spec, &workspace, release, expanded_td.as_deref())?;
   126→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?
   127→    } else {
   128→        println!("Building {} (no tspec)", pkg_name);
   129→        let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   130→        cmd.arg("build");
   131→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   132→        cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   133→        if release {
   134→            cmd.arg("--release");
   135→        }
   136→        cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?
   137→    };
   138→
   139→    // Clean up generated build.rs (only if we created it)
   140→    if !had_build_rs && build_rs_path.exists() {
   141→        let _ = fs::remove_file(&build_rs_path);
   142→    }
   143→
   144→    if !status.success() {
   145→        bail!("cargo build failed");
   146→    }
   147→
   148→    println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
   149→    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);
   150→    Ok(BuildResult {
   151→        binary_path,
   152→        target_base,
   153→    })
   154→}

   156→/// Plain `cargo build --release` with no spec lookup.
   157→/// Used by compare to produce a baseline build.
   158→pub fn plain_cargo_build_release(pkg_name: &str) -> Result<BuildResult> {
   159→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   160→    let pkg_dir = find_package_dir(&workspace, pkg_name)?;
   161→    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&pkg_dir)?;
   162→
   163→    // Clean up any stale tspec-generated build.rs from interrupted builds
   164→    let had_stale_build_rs = remove_stale_tspec_build_rs(&pkg_dir);
   165→
   166→    let binary_path = get_binary_path_simple(&workspace, &pkg_name, true);
   167→    let target_base = workspace.join("target");
   168→
   169→    println!("Building {} (no tspec)", pkg_name);
   170→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   171→    cmd.arg("build");
   172→    cmd.arg("-p").arg(&pkg_name);
   173→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   174→    cmd.arg("--release");
   175→    let status = cmd.status().context("failed to run cargo")?;
   176→
   177→    if !status.success() {
   178→        bail!("cargo build failed");
   179→    }
   180→
   181→    println!("  {}", binary_path.display());
   182→    warn_stale_build_rs(had_stale_build_rs);
   183→    Ok(BuildResult {
   184→        binary_path,
   185→        target_base,
   186→    })
   187→}
   188→
   189→/// Generate a temporary build.rs with scoped linker flags from tspec.toml
   190→pub fn generate_build_rs(path: &Path, crate_name: &str, spec: &Spec) -> Result<()> {
   191→    let mut lines = vec![TSPEC_BUILD_RS_MARKER.to_string(), "fn main() {".to_string()];
   192→
   193→    for arg in &spec.linker.args {
   194→        lines.push(format!(
   195→            "    println!(\"cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin={}={}\");",
   196→            crate_name, arg
   197→        ));
   198→    }
   199→
   200→    lines.push("}".to_string());
   201→    fs::write(path, lines.join("\n")).context("failed to write build.rs")?;
   202→    Ok(())
   203→}
   204→
   205→/// Check if spec requires nightly toolchain
   206→fn requires_nightly(spec: &Spec) -> bool {
   207→    // High-level panic mode may require nightly
   208→    let panic_needs_nightly = spec.panic.map(|p| p.requires_nightly()).unwrap_or(false);
   209→
   210→    // BuildStd requires nightly
   211→    let has_build_std = !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty();
   212→
   213→    // Unstable cargo flags require nightly
   214→    let has_unstable = !spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty();
   215→
   216→    panic_needs_nightly || has_build_std || has_unstable
   217→}
   218→
   219→/// Build the base cargo command (with toolchain if needed)
   220→fn build_cargo_command(spec: &Spec) -> Result<Command> {
   221→    let mut cmd = Command::new("cargo");
   222→
   223→    if requires_nightly(spec) {
   224→        cmd.arg("+nightly");
   225→    }
   226→
   227→    Ok(cmd)
   228→}
   229→
   230→/// Apply spec parameters to a cargo command
   231→pub fn apply_spec_to_command(
   232→    cmd: &mut Command,
   233→    spec: &Spec,
   234→    workspace: &Path,
   235→    release: bool,
   236→    expanded_target_dir: Option<&str>,
   237→) -> Result<()> {
   238→    // Set custom target directory if specified
   239→    if let Some(td) = expanded_target_dir {
   240→        cmd.arg("--target-dir")
   241→            .arg(workspace.join("target").join(td));
   242→    }
   243→
   244→    // Handle high-level panic mode (cargo -Z flag)
   245→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   246→        && let Some(z_flag) = panic_mode.cargo_z_flag()
   247→    {
   248→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(z_flag);
   249→    }
   250→
   251→    // Handle cargo config
   252→    let has_profile = spec.cargo.profile.is_some();
   253→    if let Some(ref profile) = spec.cargo.profile {
   254→        match profile {
   255→            Profile::Release => {
   256→                cmd.arg("--release");
   257→            }
   258→            Profile::Debug => {
   259→                // Debug is default, no flag needed
   260→            }
   261→        }
   262→    }
   263→
   264→    if let Some(ref triple) = spec.cargo.target_triple {
   265→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(triple);
   266→    }
   267→
   268→    if let Some(ref path) = spec.cargo.target_json {
   269→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg("json-target-spec");
   270→        cmd.arg("--target").arg(path);
   271→    }
   272→
   273→    for flag in &spec.cargo.unstable {
   274→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(flag);
   275→    }
   276→
   277→    // Inject --config key=value pairs
   278→    for (key, value) in &spec.cargo.config_key_value {
   279→        cmd.arg("--config").arg(format!("{}={}", key, value));
   280→    }
   281→
   282→    // If no profile in spec but release flag passed, use release
   283→    if !has_profile && release {
   284→        cmd.arg("--release");
   285→    }
   286→
   287→    // Collect rustc flags
   288→    let mut rustc_flags: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   289→
   290→    // Handle high-level panic mode (rustc -C flag)
   291→    if let Some(panic_mode) = spec.panic
   292→        && let Some(panic_value) = panic_mode.rustc_panic_value()
   293→    {
   294→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C panic={}", panic_value));
   295→    }
   296→
   297→    // Handle high-level strip mode
   298→    if let Some(strip_mode) = spec.strip
   299→        && let Some(strip_value) = strip_mode.rustc_strip_value()
   300→    {
   301→        rustc_flags.push(format!("-C strip={}", strip_value));
   302→    }
   303→
   304→    if !spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty() {
   305→        // -Z build-std is a cargo flag, not rustc
   306→        let crates_str = spec.cargo.build_std.join(",");
   307→        cmd.arg("-Z").arg(format!("build-std={}", crates_str));
   308→    }
   309→
   310→    for flag in &spec.rustflags {
   311→        rustc_flags.push(flag.clone());
   312→    }
   313→
   314→    // Handle version script (generates file and adds linker arg)
   315→    if let Some(vs) = &spec.linker.version_script {
   316→        let vs_dir = match expanded_target_dir {
   317→            Some(td) => workspace.join("target").join(td),
   318→            None => workspace.join("target"),
   319→        };
   320→        let _ = fs::create_dir_all(&vs_dir);
   321→        let version_script_path = vs_dir.join("tspec-version.script");
   322→
   323→        // Generate version script: { global: sym1; sym2; local: *; };
   324→        let globals = vs.global.join("; ");
   325→        let content = format!("{{ global: {}; local: {}; }};", globals, vs.local);
   326→
   327→        let mut f =
   328→            fs::File::create(&version_script_path).context("failed to create version script")?;
   329→        writeln!(f, "{}", content)?;
   330→
   331→        rustc_flags.push(format!(
   332→            "-C link-arg=-Wl,--version-script={}",
   333→            version_script_path.display()
   334→        ));
   335→    }
   336→
   337→    // Apply rustc flags (linker args from Args handled by generated build.rs)
   338→    if !rustc_flags.is_empty() {
   339→        cmd.env("RUSTFLAGS", rustc_flags.join(" "));
   340→    }
   341→
   342→    Ok(())
   343→}
   344→

     1→//! toml_edit helpers for surgical tspec editing that preserves comments/formatting.
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Result, bail};
     4→use toml_edit::{Array, DocumentMut, Item, Value};
     5→
     6→/// Whether a field holds a scalar, an array, or a table.
     7→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
     8→pub enum FieldKind {
     9→    Scalar,
    10→    Array,
    11→    Table,
    12→}
    13→
    14→/// Registry entry: (dotted key path, kind).
    15→const FIELD_REGISTRY: &[(&str, FieldKind)] = &[
    16→    ("panic", FieldKind::Scalar),
    17→    ("strip", FieldKind::Scalar),
    18→    ("cargo.profile", FieldKind::Scalar),
    19→    ("cargo.target_triple", FieldKind::Scalar),
    20→    ("cargo.target_json", FieldKind::Scalar),
    21→    ("cargo.target_dir", FieldKind::Scalar),
    22→    ("cargo.unstable", FieldKind::Array),
    23→    ("cargo.config_key_value", FieldKind::Table),
    24→    ("cargo.build_std", FieldKind::Array),
    25→    ("rustflags", FieldKind::Array),
    26→    ("linker.args", FieldKind::Array),
    27→];
    28→
    29→/// Validate that a key is in the registry and return its kind.
    30→/// Also accepts table sub-keys like `cargo.config_key_value."profile.release.opt-level"`.
    31→pub fn validate_key(key: &str) -> Result<FieldKind> {
    32→    for &(k, kind) in FIELD_REGISTRY {
    33→        if k == key {
    34→            return Ok(kind);
    35→        }
    36→    }
    37→    // Check if it's a table sub-key
    38→    if parse_table_key(key).is_some() {
    39→        return Ok(FieldKind::Table);
    40→    }
    41→    let valid_keys: Vec<&str> = FIELD_REGISTRY.iter().map(|(k, _)| *k).collect();
    42→    bail!(
    43→        "unknown key: {} (valid keys: {})",
    44→        key,
    45→        valid_keys.join(", ")
    46→    )
    47→}
    48→
    49→/// Parse a key that may reference a sub-key within a Table field.
    50→/// E.g., `cargo.config_key_value."profile.release.opt-level"` → Some(("cargo.config_key_value", "profile.release.opt-level"))
    51→/// Also accepts unquoted sub-keys: `cargo.config_key_value.profile.release.opt-level` → same result.
    52→/// Returns None if the key doesn't start with a known Table field prefix.
    53→pub fn parse_table_key(key: &str) -> Option<(&str, &str)> {
    54→    for &(prefix, kind) in FIELD_REGISTRY {
    55→        if kind != FieldKind::Table {
    56→            continue;
    57→        }
    58→        if let Some(rest) = key.strip_prefix(prefix)
    59→            && let Some(sub_key) = rest.strip_prefix('.')
    60→        {
    61→            if sub_key.is_empty() {
    62→                return None;
    63→            }
    64→            // Strip surrounding quotes if present
    65→            let sub_key = sub_key
    66→                .strip_prefix('"')
    67→                .and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix('"'))
    68→                .unwrap_or(sub_key);
    69→            if sub_key.is_empty() {
    70→                return None;
    71→            }
    72→            return Some((prefix, sub_key));
    73→        }
    74→    }
    75→    None
    76→}
    77→
    78→/// Validate a value for enum-constrained fields.
    79→/// For unconstrained fields (strings, arrays), accepts anything.
    80→pub fn validate_value(key: &str, value: &str) -> Result<()> {
    81→    match key {
    82→        "panic" => match value {
    83→            "unwind" | "abort" | "immediate-abort" => Ok(()),
    84→            _ => bail!(
    85→                "invalid panic mode: {} (expected: unwind, abort, immediate-abort)",
    86→                value
    87→            ),
    88→        },
    89→        "strip" => match value {
    90→            "none" | "debuginfo" | "symbols" => Ok(()),
    91→            _ => bail!(
    92→                "invalid strip mode: {} (expected: none, debuginfo, symbols)",
    93→                value
    94→            ),
    95→        },
    96→        "cargo.profile" => match value {
    97→            "debug" | "release" => Ok(()),
    98→            _ => bail!("invalid profile: {} (expected: debug, release)", value),
    99→        },
   100→        _ => Ok(()),
   101→    }
   102→}
   103→
   104→/// Parse a key into (optional table, field).
   105→/// "panic" -> (None, "panic")
   106→/// "rustc.lto" -> (Some("rustc"), "lto")
   107→fn parse_key(key: &str) -> (Option<&str>, &str) {
   108→    match key.split_once('.') {
   109→        Some((table, field)) => (Some(table), field),
   110→        None => (None, key),
   111→    }
   112→}
   113→
   114→/// Parse a value string into a toml_edit Value.
   115→/// All scalar fields are stored as strings.
   116→fn parse_scalar_value(_key: &str, raw: &str) -> Value {
   117→    Value::from(raw)
   118→}
   119→
   120→/// Get the existing array for a field, or an empty array if it doesn't exist.
   121→fn get_existing_array(doc: &DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Array {
   122→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   123→    match table_name {
   124→        Some(table) => doc
   125→            .get(table)
   126→            .and_then(|t| t.get(field))
   127→            .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
   128→            .cloned()
   129→            .unwrap_or_default(),
   130→        None => doc
   131→            .get(field)
   132→            .and_then(|v| v.as_array())
   133→            .cloned()
   134→            .unwrap_or_default(),
   135→    }
   136→}
   137→
   138→/// Set an array value into the document at the given key path.
   139→fn set_array_in_doc(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, arr: Array) {
   140→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   141→    match table_name {
   142→        Some(table) => {
   143→            ensure_table(doc, table);
   144→            doc[table][field] = Item::Value(Value::Array(arr));
   145→        }
   146→        None => {
   147→            doc[field] = Item::Value(Value::Array(arr));
   148→        }
   149→    }
   150→}
   151→
   152→/// Ensure a table exists in the document.
   153→fn ensure_table(doc: &mut DocumentMut, table: &str) {
   154→    if doc.get(table).is_none() {
   155→        doc[table] = Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   156→    }
   157→}
   158→
   159→// === Public API: takes &[String] from shell args, no string parsing ===
   160→
   161→/// Set a field from string args. Scalars take `values[0]`, arrays take all values.
   162→pub fn set_field(
   163→    doc: &mut DocumentMut,
   164→    key: &str,
   165→    values: &[String],
   166→    kind: FieldKind,
   167→) -> Result<()> {
   168→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   169→
   170→    match kind {
   171→        FieldKind::Scalar => {
   172→            if values.len() != 1 {
   173→                bail!(
   174→                    "scalar field '{}' requires exactly one value, got {}",
   175→                    key,
   176→                    values.len()
   177→                );
   178→            }
   179→            let val = parse_scalar_value(key, &values[0]);
   180→            match table_name {
   181→                Some(table) => {
   182→                    ensure_table(doc, table);
   183→                    doc[table][field] = Item::Value(val);
   184→                }
   185→                None => {
   186→                    doc[field] = Item::Value(val);
   187→                }
   188→            }
   189→        }
   190→        FieldKind::Array => {
   191→            let mut arr = Array::new();
   192→            for v in values {
   193→                arr.push(v.as_str());
   194→            }
   195→            set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);
   196→        }
   197→        FieldKind::Table => {
   198→            bail!(
   199→                "use set_table_value() for table field '{}'; set_field() does not handle tables",
   200→                key
   201→            );
   202→        }
   203→    }
   204→
   205→    Ok(())
   206→}
   207→
   208→/// Add items to an array field. Appends by default, or inserts at `index`.
   209→/// Deduplicates on append; insert adds at position without dedup.
   210→pub fn add_items(
   211→    doc: &mut DocumentMut,
   212→    key: &str,
   213→    values: &[String],
   214→    index: Option<usize>,
   215→) -> Result<()> {
   216→    let mut arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   217→
   218→    match index {
   219→        Some(idx) => {
   220→            if idx > arr.len() {
   221→                bail!(
   222→                    "index {} out of bounds for array '{}' with {} elements",
   223→                    idx,
   224→                    key,
   225→                    arr.len()
   226→                );
   227→            }
   228→            // Insert at position (no dedup — user explicitly chose position)
   229→            for (offset, v) in values.iter().enumerate() {
   230→                arr.insert(idx + offset, v.as_str());
   231→            }
   232→        }
   233→        None => {
   234→            // Append with dedup
   235→            let existing: Vec<String> = arr
   236→                .iter()
   237→                .filter_map(|v| v.as_str().map(|s| s.to_string()))
   238→                .collect();
   239→            for v in values {
   240→                if !existing.contains(v) {
   241→                    arr.push(v.as_str());
   242→                }
   243→            }
   244→        }
   245→    }
   246→
   247→    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);
   248→    Ok(())
   249→}
   250→
   251→/// Remove items from an array field by value.
   252→/// Keeps the field as an empty array if all items are removed.
   253→pub fn remove_items_by_value(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, values: &[String]) -> Result<()> {
   254→    let arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   255→
   256→    let mut new_arr = Array::new();
   257→    for item in arr.iter() {
   258→        if let Some(s) = item.as_str()
   259→            && !values.iter().any(|v| v == s)
   260→        {
   261→            new_arr.push(s);
   262→        }
   263→    }
   264→
   265→    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, new_arr);
   266→    Ok(())
   267→}
   268→
   269→/// Remove an item from an array field by index.
   270→/// Keeps the field as an empty array if the last item is removed.
   271→pub fn remove_item_by_index(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str, index: usize) -> Result<()> {
   272→    let mut arr = get_existing_array(doc, key);
   273→
   274→    if index >= arr.len() {
   275→        bail!(
   276→            "index {} out of bounds for array '{}' with {} elements",
   277→            index,
   278→            key,
   279→            arr.len()
   280→        );
   281→    }
   282→
   283→    arr.remove(index);
   284→    set_array_in_doc(doc, key, arr);
   285→    Ok(())
   286→}
   287→
   288→/// Remove a field from a toml_edit document.
   289→/// Does not remove the containing table, even if it becomes empty.
   290→pub fn unset_field(doc: &mut DocumentMut, key: &str) -> Result<()> {
   291→    let (table_name, field) = parse_key(key);
   292→
   293→    match table_name {
   294→        Some(table) => {
   295→            if let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = doc.get_mut(table) {
   296→                tbl.remove(field);
   297→            }
   298→        }
   299→        None => {
   300→            doc.remove(field);
   301→        }
   302→    }
   303→
   304→    Ok(())
   305→}
   306→
   307→/// Smart-parse a raw value string into a toml_edit Value for table entries.
   308→/// Booleans → bool, integers → i64, everything else → string.
   309→fn parse_smart_value(raw: &str) -> Value {
   310→    match raw {
   311→        "true" => Value::from(true),
   312→        "false" => Value::from(false),
   313→        _ => {
   314→            if let Ok(n) = raw.parse::<i64>() {
   315→                Value::from(n)
   316→            } else {
   317→                Value::from(raw)
   318→            }
   319→        }
   320→    }
   321→}
   322→
   323→/// Set a value in a table field (e.g., `cargo.config_key_value`).
   324→/// `table_path` is the dotted path to the table (e.g., "cargo.config_key_value").
   325→/// `sub_key` is the key within that table (e.g., "profile.release.opt-level").
   326→/// `raw_value` is the string value to set (auto-parsed to bool/int/string).
   327→pub fn set_table_value(
   328→    doc: &mut DocumentMut,
   329→    table_path: &str,
   330→    sub_key: &str,
   331→    raw_value: &str,
   332→) -> Result<()> {
   333→    let (parent, table_name) = parse_key(table_path);
   334→    let val = parse_smart_value(raw_value);
   335→
   336→    match parent {
   337→        Some(p) => {
   338→            ensure_table(doc, p);
   339→            // Ensure the nested table exists
   340→            if doc[p].get(table_name).is_none() {
   341→                doc[p][table_name] = Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   342→            }
   343→            doc[p][table_name][sub_key] = Item::Value(val);
   344→        }
   345→        None => {
   346→            if doc.get(table_name).is_none() {
   347→                doc[table_name] = Item::Table(toml_edit::Table::new());
   348→            }
   349→            doc[table_name][sub_key] = Item::Value(val);
   350→        }
   351→    }
   352→
   353→    Ok(())
   354→}
   355→
   356→/// Remove a single key from a table field.
   357→pub fn unset_table_value(doc: &mut DocumentMut, table_path: &str, sub_key: &str) -> Result<()> {
   358→    let (parent, table_name) = parse_key(table_path);
   359→
   360→    match parent {
   361→        Some(p) => {
   362→            if let Some(Item::Table(parent_tbl)) = doc.get_mut(p)
   363→                && let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = parent_tbl.get_mut(table_name)
   364→            {
   365→                tbl.remove(sub_key);
   366→            }
   367→        }
   368→        None => {
   369→            if let Some(Item::Table(tbl)) = doc.get_mut(table_name) {
   370→                tbl.remove(sub_key);
   371→            }
   372→        }
   373→    }
   374→
   375→    Ok(())
   376→}

   378→#[cfg(test)]
   379→mod tests {
   380→    use super::*;
   381→
   382→    // Helper to make a Vec<String> from string literals
   383→    fn vs(items: &[&str]) -> Vec<String> {
   384→        items.iter().map(|s| s.to_string()).collect()
   385→    }
   386→
   387→    #[test]
   388→    fn validate_key_valid_scalar() {
   389→        assert_eq!(validate_key("panic").unwrap(), FieldKind::Scalar);
   390→        assert_eq!(validate_key("cargo.profile").unwrap(), FieldKind::Scalar);
   391→    }
   392→
   393→    #[test]
   394→    fn validate_key_valid_array() {

   397→        assert_eq!(validate_key("cargo.unstable").unwrap(), FieldKind::Array);
   398→        assert_eq!(validate_key("rustflags").unwrap(), FieldKind::Array);
   399→    }
   400→
   401→    #[test]

     1→//! `tspec ts set` - Set a value in a tspec using toml_edit (preserves comments/formatting)
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→use toml_edit::DocumentMut;
     6→
     7→use super::edit::{self, FieldKind};
     8→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     9→
    10→/// Set a field in a tspec (scalar or replace entire array) and save in place
    11→pub fn set_value(
    12→    project_root: &Path,
    13→    package: Option<&str>,
    14→    key: &str,
    15→    values: &[String],
    16→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    17→) -> Result<()> {
    18→    let workspace = project_root;
    19→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    20→
    21→    // Resolve tspec path (existing or new)
    22→    let output_path = match find_tspec(&package_dir, tspec)? {
    23→        Some(path) => path,
    24→        None => {
    25→            let base_name = match tspec {
    26→                Some(t) => t
    27→                    .strip_suffix(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX)
    28→                    .or_else(|| t.strip_suffix(".toml"))
    29→                    .unwrap_or(t),
    30→                None => "tspec",
    31→            };
    32→            package_dir.join(format!("{}{}", base_name, crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX))
    33→        }
    34→    };
    35→
    36→    // Validate key and value
    37→    let kind = edit::validate_key(key)?;
    38→
    39→    // Handle Table fields with sub-keys
    40→    if kind == FieldKind::Table {
    41→        if let Some((table_path, sub_key)) = edit::parse_table_key(key) {
    42→            if values.len() != 1 {
    43→                anyhow::bail!(
    44→                    "table sub-key '{}' requires exactly one value, got {}",
    45→                    key,
    46→                    values.len()
    47→                );
    48→            }
    49→
    50→            let content = if output_path.exists() {
    51→                std::fs::read_to_string(&output_path)
    52→                    .with_context(|| format!("failed to read: {}", output_path.display()))?
    53→            } else {
    54→                String::new()
    55→            };
    56→
    57→            let mut doc: DocumentMut = content
    58→                .parse()
    59→                .with_context(|| format!("failed to parse: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    60→
    61→            edit::set_table_value(&mut doc, table_path, sub_key, &values[0])?;
    62→
    63→            std::fs::write(&output_path, doc.to_string())
    64→                .with_context(|| format!("failed to write: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    65→
    66→            println!(
    67→                "Saved {}",
    68→                output_path
    69→                    .strip_prefix(workspace)
    70→                    .unwrap_or(&output_path)
    71→                    .display()
    72→            );
    73→            return Ok(());
    74→        } else {
    75→            anyhow::bail!(
    76→                "'ts set' on table field '{}' requires a sub-key, e.g. {}.\"key\"",
    77→                key,
    78→                key
    79→            );
    80→        }
    81→    }
    82→
    83→    // Validate enum constraints for scalar fields
    84→    if kind == FieldKind::Scalar {
    85→        if values.len() != 1 {
    86→            anyhow::bail!(
    87→                "scalar field '{}' requires exactly one value, got {}",
    88→                key,
    89→                values.len()
    90→            );
    91→        }
    92→        edit::validate_value(key, &values[0])?;
    93→    }
    94→
    95→    // Read existing content or start empty
    96→    let content = if output_path.exists() {
    97→        std::fs::read_to_string(&output_path)
    98→            .with_context(|| format!("failed to read: {}", output_path.display()))?
    99→    } else {
   100→        String::new()
   101→    };
   102→
   103→    // Parse, edit, write
   104→    let mut doc: DocumentMut = content
   105→        .parse()
   106→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to parse: {}", output_path.display()))?;
   107→
   108→    edit::set_field(&mut doc, key, values, kind)?;
   109→
   110→    std::fs::write(&output_path, doc.to_string())
   111→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write: {}", output_path.display()))?;
   112→
   113→    println!(
   114→        "Saved {}",
   115→        output_path
   116→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
   117→            .unwrap_or(&output_path)
   118→            .display()
   119→    );
   120→
   121→    Ok(())
   122→}

     1→//! `tspec ts unset` - Remove a field from a tspec (preserves comments/formatting)
     2→
     3→use anyhow::{Context, Result, bail};
     4→use std::path::Path;
     5→use toml_edit::DocumentMut;
     6→
     7→use super::edit;
     8→use crate::find_paths::{find_tspec, resolve_package_dir};
     9→
    10→/// Remove a field from a tspec
    11→pub fn unset_value(
    12→    project_root: &Path,
    13→    package: Option<&str>,
    14→    key: &str,
    15→    tspec: Option<&str>,
    16→) -> Result<()> {
    17→    let workspace = project_root;
    18→    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(workspace, package)?;
    19→
    20→    let output_path = match find_tspec(&package_dir, tspec)? {
    21→        Some(path) => path,
    22→        None => bail!("no tspec found to modify"),
    23→    };
    24→
    25→    // Validate the key
    26→    let kind = edit::validate_key(key)?;
    27→
    28→    // Read, parse, edit, write
    29→    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&output_path)
    30→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    31→
    32→    let mut doc: DocumentMut = content
    33→        .parse()
    34→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to parse: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    35→
    36→    if kind == edit::FieldKind::Table {
    37→        if let Some((table_path, sub_key)) = edit::parse_table_key(key) {
    38→            edit::unset_table_value(&mut doc, table_path, sub_key)?;
    39→        } else {
    40→            // Bare table name (e.g., "cargo.config_key_value") — remove entire table
    41→            edit::unset_field(&mut doc, key)?;
    42→        }
    43→    } else {
    44→        edit::unset_field(&mut doc, key)?;
    45→    }
    46→
    47→    std::fs::write(&output_path, doc.to_string())
    48→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write: {}", output_path.display()))?;
    49→
    50→    println!(
    51→        "Saved {}",
    52→        output_path
    53→            .strip_prefix(workspace)
    54→            .unwrap_or(&output_path)
    55→            .display()
    56→    );
    57→
    58→    Ok(())
    59→}

    17→    assert_eq!(spec.panic, Some(PanicMode::Abort));
    18→
    19→    assert_eq!(spec.cargo.profile, Some(Profile::Release));
    20→    assert!(spec.cargo.target_triple.is_none());
    21→    assert!(spec.cargo.target_json.is_none());
    22→    assert!(spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty());
    23→    assert_eq!(
    24→        spec.cargo.config_key_value.get("profile.release.opt-level"),
    25→        Some(&ConfigValue::String("z".to_string()))
    26→    );
    27→
    28→    assert!(spec.cargo.build_std.is_empty());
    29→    assert!(spec.rustflags.is_empty());
    30→
    31→    assert_eq!(spec.linker, LinkerConfig::default());
    32→}
    33→
    34→#[test]
    35→fn load_spec_file_not_found() {
    36→    let result = load_spec(&test_data("nonexistent.toml"));
    37→    assert!(result.is_err());
    38→}
    39→
    40→#[test]
    41→fn loaded_spec_hash_is_stable() {
    42→    let spec = load_spec(&test_data("minimal.toml")).unwrap();
    43→    let hash1 = hash_spec(&spec).unwrap();
    44→    let hash2 = hash_spec(&spec).unwrap();
    45→    assert_eq!(hash1, hash2);
    46→}
    47→
    48→#[test]
    49→fn load_ex_x1_spec() {
    50→    let spec = load_spec(&test_data("ex-x1.ts.toml")).unwrap();
    51→
    52→    assert_eq!(spec.cargo, CargoConfig::default());
    53→    assert!(spec.rustflags.is_empty());
    54→    assert_eq!(
    55→        spec.linker.args,
    56→        vec!["-static".to_string(), "-nostartfiles".to_string()]
    57→    );
    58→    assert!(spec.linker.version_script.is_none());
    59→}
    60→
    61→#[test]
    62→fn load_ex_x2_spec() {
    63→    let spec = load_spec(&test_data("ex-x2.ts.toml")).unwrap();
    64→
    65→    assert_eq!(spec.cargo, CargoConfig::default());
    66→    assert!(spec.rustflags.is_empty());
    67→    assert_eq!(
    68→        spec.linker.args,
    69→        vec![
    70→            "-static".to_string(),
    71→            "-nostdlib".to_string(),
    72→            "-nodefaultlibs".to_string(),
    73→            "-e_start".to_string(),
    74→            "-Wl,--undefined=_start".to_string(),
    75→            "-Wl,--undefined=__libc_start_main".to_string(),
    76→        ]
    77→    );
    78→    assert!(spec.linker.version_script.is_none());
    79→}
    80→
    81→#[test]
    82→fn load_ex_config_kv_spec() {
    83→    let spec = load_spec(&test_data("ex-config-kv.ts.toml")).unwrap();
    84→
    85→    assert_eq!(spec.cargo.profile, Some(Profile::Release));
    86→
    87→    let expected = BTreeMap::from([
    88→        (
    89→            "profile.release.opt-level".to_string(),
    90→            ConfigValue::String("s".to_string()),
    91→        ),
    92→        ("profile.release.lto".to_string(), ConfigValue::Bool(true)),
    93→        (
    94→            "profile.release.codegen-units".to_string(),
    95→            ConfigValue::Integer(1),
    96→        ),
    97→    ]);
    98→    assert_eq!(spec.cargo.config_key_value, expected);
    99→}
   100→
   101→#[test]
   102→fn config_kv_hash_is_stable() {
   103→    let spec = load_spec(&test_data("ex-config-kv.ts.toml")).unwrap();
   104→    let hash1 = hash_spec(&spec).unwrap();
   105→    let hash2 = hash_spec(&spec).unwrap();

   113→    let hash_kv = hash_spec(&spec_kv).unwrap();

    62→build_std = ["core", "alloc"]              # crates to rebuild with -Z build-std (nightly)

    75→```
    76→
    77→The top-level `panic` sets both cargo `-Z` and rustc `-C` flags automatically.
    78→
    79→### Key Concepts
    80→
    81→- **One tspec per package** - A tspec applies to all crates (targets) within a Cargo package. For per-crate control, use separate packages in a workspace.
    82→- **tspec.ts.toml is optional** - Packages without one get plain `cargo build/test`
    83→- **Generated build.rs** - tspec generates temporary build.rs for scoped linker flags
    84→- **Spec comparison** - Compare binary sizes across different specs; always shows both `cargo --release` and `cargo --release-strip` baselines. Supports `-w` for all-packages mode — per-package tables are collected and printed together at the end. With a single package, only its comparison table is shown
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/types.rs",
  "old_string": "/// A value in the `[cargo.config_key_value]` table.\n/// Uses `#[serde(untagged)]` so TOML bools/ints/strings are deserialized naturally.\n/// We avoid `toml::Value` because it contains `Float(f64)` which doesn't implement `Eq`.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(untagged)]\npub enum ConfigValue {\n    Bool(bool),\n    Integer(i64),\n    String(String),\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for ConfigValue {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            ConfigValue::Bool(b) => write!(f, \"{}\", b),\n            ConfigValue::Integer(n) => write!(f, \"{}\", n),\n            ConfigValue::String(s) => write!(f, \"\\\"{}\\\"\", s),\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// A value in the `[cargo.config]` table.\n/// Uses `#[serde(untagged)]` so TOML bools/ints/strings/tables are deserialized naturally.\n/// We avoid `toml::Value` because it contains `Float(f64)` which doesn't implement `Eq`.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(untagged)]\npub enum ConfigValue {\n    Bool(bool),\n    Integer(i64),\n    String(String),\n    Table(BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>),\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for ConfigValue {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            ConfigValue::Bool(b) => write!(f, \"{}\", b),\n            ConfigValue::Integer(n) => write!(f, \"{}\", n),\n            ConfigValue::String(s) => write!(f, \"\\\"{}\\\"\", s),\n            ConfigValue::Table(map) => write!(f, \"{:?}\", map),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Flatten nested config into dotted key-value pairs for --config args.\npub fn flatten_config(config: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>) -> Vec<(String, String)> {\n    let mut result = Vec::new();\n    flatten_inner(config, String::new(), &mut result);\n    result\n}\n\nfn flatten_inner(\n    map: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>,\n    prefix: String,\n    result: &mut Vec<(String, String)>,\n) {\n    for (key, value) in map {\n        let full_key = if prefix.is_empty() {\n            key.clone()\n        } else {\n            format!(\"{}.{}\", prefix, key)\n        };\n        match value {\n            ConfigValue::Table(inner) => flatten_inner(inner, full_key, result),\n            other => result.push((full_key, other.to_string())),\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

     1→use std::collections::BTreeMap;

     6→
     7→fn test_data(name: &str) -> PathBuf {

     9→        .join("tests/data")

    13→#[test]
    14→fn load_minimal_spec() {
    15→    let spec = load_spec(&test_data("minimal.toml")).unwrap();
    16→
    17→    assert_eq!(spec.panic, Some(PanicMode::Abort));
    18→
    19→    assert_eq!(spec.cargo.profile, Some(Profile::Release));
    20→    assert!(spec.cargo.target_triple.is_none());
    21→    assert!(spec.cargo.target_json.is_none());
    22→    assert!(spec.cargo.unstable.is_empty());

    24→        spec.cargo.config.get("profile.release.opt-level"),
    25→        Some(&ConfigValue::String("z".to_string()))
    26→    );

    30→
    31→    assert_eq!(spec.linker, LinkerConfig::default());

    33→

I see — the `set_table_value` function takes `table_path = "cargo.config"` and parses it with `parse_key` which gives `(Some("cargo"), "config")`. So the toml_edit document key is `doc["cargo"]["config"]`, not `doc["cargo"]["config_key_value"]`. These assertions have a bug (they would have failed even before my change if the table_path was already "cargo.config"). They must have been left over. Let me fix them.

All checks pass. Here's a summary of what was done:

**Changes made:**

1. **`src/types.rs`** — Added `Table(BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>)` variant to `ConfigValue`, renamed `config_key_value` field to `config` in `CargoConfig`, added `Display` for `Table`, added `flatten_config()` function that recursively walks nested tables to produce dotted key-value pairs for `--config` args.

2. **`src/cargo_build.rs`** — Updated to use `flatten_config(&spec.cargo.config)` instead of iterating `config_key_value` directly. Renamed tests, added `config_nested_table_emits_config_args` test for nested table support.

3. **`src/ts_cmd/edit.rs`** — Updated field registry from `cargo.config_key_value` to `cargo.config`, updated all doc comments and test assertions.

4. **`src/ts_cmd/set.rs`** — Updated all `config_key_value` references to `config`.

5. **`src/ts_cmd/unset.rs`** — Updated all `config_key_value` references to `config`.

6. **`tests/tspec_test.rs`** — Updated to use `flatten_config()` for assertions since spec files now use nested tables.

7. **Spec files** — Converted all `[cargo.config_key_value]` sections to nested `[cargo.config.profile.release]` form:
   - `tspec.release.ts.toml`, `tspec.musl.ts.toml`, `tests/data/ex-config-kv.ts.toml`, `tests/data/minimal.toml`, and all 4 `tspec-build/*.ts.toml` files.

8. **Documentation** — Updated `README.md` (spec example and supported keys table) and `CLAUDE.md` (Translation Spec Structure section).

Remember to commit `.claude/` session files.

> DEVELOPER

Is `profile.release` is just a "section" and it could be "profile.xyz" or "xyz.def"?

> AGENT

Yes, exactly. `cargo.config` is a pure passthrough to `cargo --config KEY=VALUE` — the keys can be anything cargo accepts. `profile.release` is just the most common example. You could equally write:

```toml
[cargo.config.profile.dev]
opt-level = 2

[cargo.config.build]
rustflags = ["-C", "target-cpu=native"]

[cargo.config.target.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu]
linker = "clang"
```

`flatten_config()` just walks whatever nesting is there and produces dotted keys — it doesn't validate or restrict them. Cargo itself handles validation when it receives the `--config` args.

> AGENT

Here's the current situation:

1. `cargo.profile` is an enum: only `"debug"` or `"release"`. Setting `cargo.profile = "abc"` would fail at deserialization. So that specific case can't happen.

2. The real issue is **consistency** — nothing prevents you from writing:
   ```toml
   [cargo]
   profile = "debug"
   
   [cargo.config.profile.release]
   opt-level = "z"
   ```
   This would build in debug mode while passing `--config profile.release.opt-level="z"` — the config values would be accepted by cargo but have no effect on the actual build (since release profile isn't active). No error, just silently ignored settings.

3. For **multiple profiles in one tspec** — all `cargo.config.profile.*` entries get passed every time. Cargo only applies the active profile's settings, so the others are harmless but wasteful.

As for the dedicated section — should it:
- Just document the passthrough mechanism with varied examples (`profile`, `build`, `target`, etc.)?
- Or are you also thinking about a future feature where tspec supports custom profiles / profile selection at the CLI level (like `tspec build --profile custom`)?

The latter would be a bigger design discussion since `cargo.profile` is currently limited to debug/release.

> DEVELOPER

Just document the passthrough mechanism with varied examples for now, but also document that only `cargo.config.profile.debug` and `carog.config.profile.release` are valid. Is it easy to have tspec error if something other than  "profile.release" or "profile.debug" is present. Also, add a quick Todo for "custom profiles / profile selection".

```
    83→- **Generated build.rs** - tspec generates temporary build.rs for scoped linker flags
    84→- **Spec comparison** - Compare binary sizes across different specs; always shows both `cargo --release` and `cargo --release-strip` baselines. Supports `-w` for all-packages mode — per-package tables are collected and printed together at the end. With a single package, only its comparison table is shown
    85→
    86→### How Linker Flags Work
    87→
    88→tspec generates a temporary `build.rs` to apply linker flags with binary-scoped directives:
    89→
    90→```rust
    91→// Generated by tspec - deleted after build
    92→fn main() {
    93→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-static");
    94→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-nostdlib");
    95→}
    96→```
    97→
    98→This is necessary because RUSTFLAGS affects ALL compilations (including dependency
    99→build scripts), causing crashes with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated
   100→build.rs scopes flags to just the binary.
   101→
   102→## tspec Management (ts subcommand)
   103→
   104→Manage spec files without manual TOML editing. All commands that modify files preserve comments and formatting.
   105→
   106→| Command | Description |
   107→|---------|-------------|
   108→| `ts list` | List tspec files found in the workspace or a specific package |
   109→| `ts show` | Display the TOML contents of a tspec file |
   110→| `ts hash` | Print the content hash (used in backup filenames) of a tspec |
   111→| `ts new` | Create a new tspec with defaults, or copy an existing one with `-f` (byte-for-byte) |
   112→| `ts set` | Set a scalar value or replace an entire array |
```

    40→pub fn flatten_config(config: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    41→    let mut result = Vec::new();
    42→    flatten_inner(config, String::new(), &mut result);
    43→    result
    44→}
    45→
    46→fn flatten_inner(
    47→    map: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>,
    48→    prefix: String,
    49→    result: &mut Vec<(String, String)>,
    50→) {
    51→    for (key, value) in map {
    52→        let full_key = if prefix.is_empty() {
    53→            key.clone()
    54→        } else {
    55→            format!("{}.{}", prefix, key)
    56→        };
    57→        match value {
    58→            ConfigValue::Table(inner) => flatten_inner(inner, full_key, result),
    59→            other => result.push((full_key, other.to_string())),
    60→        }
    61→    }
    62→}
    63→
    64→/// Cargo-specific configuration (flat struct)
    65→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    66→pub struct CargoConfig {
    67→    /// Build profile (debug or release)
    68→    pub profile: Option<Profile>,
    69→    /// Target triple (e.g., "x86_64-unknown-linux-musl")
    70→    pub target_triple: Option<String>,
    71→    /// Custom target JSON file path
    72→    pub target_json: Option<PathBuf>,
    73→    /// Nightly-only -Z flags (e.g., ["panic-immediate-abort"])
    74→    #[serde(default)]
    75→    pub unstable: Vec<String>,
    76→    /// Custom target directory subdirectory for per-spec isolation.
    77→    /// Supports `{name}` (spec filename sans .ts.toml) and `{hash}` (8-char content hash).
    78→    pub target_dir: Option<String>,
    79→    /// Config values passed as `--config 'KEY=VALUE'` to cargo.

  "old_string": "/// Flatten nested config into dotted key-value pairs for --config args.\npub fn flatten_config(config: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>) -> Vec<(String, String)> {",
  "new_string": "/// Validate that config profile keys are only \"debug\" or \"release\".\n/// Returns an error if e.g. `profile.custom` is found.\npub fn validate_config_profiles(config: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>) -> Result<(), String> {\n    if let Some(ConfigValue::Table(profiles)) = config.get(\"profile\") {\n        for name in profiles.keys() {\n            if name != \"debug\" && name != \"release\" {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"unsupported profile in [cargo.config]: \\\"{}\\\" (only \\\"debug\\\" and \\\"release\\\" are supported)\",\n                    name\n                ));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    // Also check flat dotted keys like \"profile.foo.opt-level\"\n    for key in config.keys() {\n        if let Some(rest) = key.strip_prefix(\"profile.\") {\n            let profile_name = rest.split('.').next().unwrap_or(rest);\n            if profile_name != \"debug\" && profile_name != \"release\" {\n                return Err(format!(\n                    \"unsupported profile in [cargo.config]: \\\"{}\\\" (only \\\"debug\\\" and \\\"release\\\" are supported)\",\n                    profile_name\n                ));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Flatten nested config into dotted key-value pairs for --config args.\npub fn flatten_config(config: &BTreeMap<String, ConfigValue>) -> Vec<(String, String)> {"
}

  "new_string": "use crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX;\nuse crate::types::{Spec, validate_config_profiles};\n\n/// Load a spec from a TOML file\npub fn load_spec(path: &Path) -> Result<Spec> {\n    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)\n        .with_context(|| format!(\"failed to read spec file: {}\", path.display()))?;\n    let spec = parse_spec(&content)?;\n    if let Err(msg) = validate_config_profiles(&spec.cargo.config) {\n        anyhow::bail!(\"{}: {}\", path.display(), msg);\n    }\n    Ok(spec)\n}\n\n/// Parse a spec from TOML string\npub fn parse_spec(toml_str: &str) -> Result<Spec> {\n    toml::from_str(toml_str).context(\"failed to parse spec TOML\")\n}"
}

    90→pub fn next_snapshot_seq(name: &str, dir: &Path) -> Result<u32> {
    91→    let prefix = format!("{}-", name);
    92→    let next_seq = std::fs::read_dir(dir)
    93→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to read directory: {}", dir.display()))?
    94→        .filter_map(|entry| entry.ok())
    95→        .filter_map(|entry| {
    96→            let filename = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned();
    97→            if filename.starts_with(&prefix) && filename.ends_with(TSPEC_SUFFIX) {
    98→                let rest = filename.strip_prefix(&prefix)?;
    99→                let seq_str = rest.split('-').next()?;
   100→                seq_str.parse::<u32>().ok()
   101→            } else {
   102→                None
   103→            }
   104→        })
   105→        .max()
   106→        .map(|n| n + 1)
   107→        .unwrap_or(1);
   108→    Ok(next_seq)
   109→}
   110→
   111→/// Save a spec snapshot with sequence number and hash: `{name}-{seq:03}-{hash}.toml`
   112→/// Returns the path of the created file.
   113→pub fn save_spec_snapshot(spec: &Spec, name: &str, dir: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf> {
   114→    std::fs::create_dir_all(dir)
   115→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to create directory: {}", dir.display()))?;
   116→
   117→    let next_seq = next_snapshot_seq(name, dir)?;
   118→    let hash = hash_spec(spec)?;
   119→    let filename = format!("{}-{:03}-{}{}", name, next_seq, hash, TSPEC_SUFFIX);
   120→    let path = dir.join(&filename);
   121→
   122→    let content = serialize_spec(spec)?;
   123→    std::fs::write(&path, &content)
   124→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to write spec file: {}", path.display()))?;
   125→
   126→    Ok(path)
   127→}
   128→
   129→/// Create a backup snapshot using raw file copy (preserves comments/formatting).
   130→/// Loads the spec only for hashing (to compute the backup filename).
   131→/// Returns the path of the created backup file.
   132→pub fn copy_spec_snapshot(source: &Path, name: &str, dir: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf> {
   133→    std::fs::create_dir_all(dir)
   134→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to create directory: {}", dir.display()))?;
   135→
   136→    let spec = load_spec(source)?;
   137→    let next_seq = next_snapshot_seq(name, dir)?;
   138→    let hash = hash_spec(&spec)?;
   139→    let filename = format!("{}-{:03}-{}{}", name, next_seq, hash, TSPEC_SUFFIX);
   140→    let path = dir.join(&filename);
   141→
   142→    std::fs::copy(source, &path)
   143→        .with_context(|| format!("failed to copy {} to {}", source.display(), path.display()))?;
   144→
   145→    Ok(path)
   146→}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/README.md",
  "old_string": "## tspec Management (ts subcommand)",
  "new_string": "### Cargo Config Passthrough (`cargo.config`)\n\nThe `[cargo.config]` section is a general-purpose passthrough to cargo's `--config KEY=VALUE` mechanism. Any nested tables under `[cargo.config]` are flattened into dotted keys and passed as `--config` args. This lets you override any [cargo configuration](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html) value at the command line.\n\n**Profile settings** are the most common use case:\n\n```toml\n[cargo.config.profile.release]\nopt-level = \"z\"\ncodegen-units = 1\nlto = true\n```\n\nThis produces: `cargo --config profile.release.opt-level=\"z\" --config profile.release.codegen-units=1 --config profile.release.lto=true`\n\n**Other cargo config keys** work too:\n\n```toml\n[cargo.config.build]\nrustflags = [\"-C\", \"target-cpu=native\"]\n\n[cargo.config.target.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu]\nlinker = \"clang\"\n```\n\n**Profile restrictions:** Only `profile.debug` and `profile.release` are supported. tspec will error if other profile names (e.g. `profile.custom`) are used in `[cargo.config]`.\n\n**Flat dotted keys** also work — TOML treats them equivalently:\n\n```toml\n[cargo.config]\n\"profile.release.opt-level\" = \"z\"\n```\n\n## tspec Management (ts subcommand)"
}
```

> AGENT

All done. Here's a summary of the additions:

**Validation** (`src/types.rs` + `src/tspec.rs`):
- Added `validate_config_profiles()` that checks both nested (`profile.custom` table key) and flat (`"profile.custom.opt-level"` dotted key) forms
- Called from `load_spec()` — errors include the file path for context
- 5 new unit tests covering: accept release, accept debug, reject custom nested, reject custom flat, accept non-profile keys

**README section** — New "Cargo Config Passthrough (`cargo.config`)" section between "How Linker Flags Work" and "tspec Management", showing:
- Profile settings example (most common case)
- Other cargo config keys (`build`, `target`)
- Profile restriction note
- Flat dotted key equivalence

**Todo** — Added: "Add custom profile support and CLI profile selection (e.g. `tspec build --profile custom`)"

Remember to commit `.claude/` session files.

    86→### How Linker Flags Work
    87→
    88→tspec generates a temporary `build.rs` to apply linker flags with binary-scoped directives:
    89→
    90→```rust
    91→// Generated by tspec - deleted after build
    92→fn main() {
    93→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-static");
    94→    println!("cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=myapp=-nostdlib");
    95→}
    96→```
    97→
    98→This is necessary because RUSTFLAGS affects ALL compilations (including dependency
    99→build scripts), causing crashes with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated
   100→build.rs scopes flags to just the binary.
   101→
   102→### Cargo Config Passthrough (`cargo.config`)
   103→
   104→The `[cargo.config]` section is a general-purpose passthrough to cargo's `--config KEY=VALUE` mechanism. Any nested tables under `[cargo.config]` are flattened into dotted keys and passed as `--config` args. This lets you override any [cargo configuration](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html) value at the command line.
   105→
```

> AGENT

Actually, tspec *does* still use RUSTFLAGS — for rustc flags like `-C panic=abort`, `-C strip=symbols`, and the top-level `rustflags` array. The paragraph is still accurate: linker flags like `-static` and `-nostartfiles` can't go in RUSTFLAGS because they'd affect all compilations, so tspec uses the generated `build.rs` with `cargo:rustc-link-arg-bin=` to scope them to just the binary.

The paragraph isn't vestigial — it explains *why* the generated build.rs approach exists instead of just putting linker args in RUSTFLAGS.

> DEVELOPER

What about; "The only other alternative is to add these linker flags to RUSTFLAGS, but that affects ALL compilations .."

> AGENT

That's clearer — it frames it as "here's why we *don't* use the obvious alternative" rather than implying we used to do it differently.

> DEVELOPER

Amended/forced-pushed, we should probably get in a consistent habit that on all non-trivial commits we should work on a branch as force pushing on the "main" release branch is really bad form. I've gotten in this bad habit because all of my current git projects are just me playing around, but this might be different. Thoughts or other techniques, I understand there are some git compatible tools that might be better?

> AGENT

You're right — force-pushing to main is a risk even solo. One accidental `--force` on a shared repo and history is gone.

Some options:

**1. Feature branches (your CLAUDE.md already prescribes this)**
The workflow in CLAUDE.md already says to create branches like `feat-rename-config`. We just haven't been following it for "quick" changes. The fix is discipline: *every* non-trivial change gets a branch, even if it feels small. Merge with `--no-ff` to keep a merge commit, or squash-merge for clean history.

**2. Protect main**
GitHub/GitLab let you protect main so force-push is blocked even for the repo owner. This is a safety net that costs nothing.

**3. `git config receive.denyNonFastForwards true`** 
Server-side rejection of force pushes. Works for self-hosted repos.

**4. `--force-with-lease` instead of `--force`**
If you ever need to force-push a feature branch, this is safer — it fails if someone else has pushed since your last fetch. Not relevant for main, but good habit for branches.

**5. Stacked-diff tools (Graphite, git-town, jj)**
- **jj (Jujutsu)** — git-compatible VCS that makes amending, rebasing, and splitting commits trivial without the footguns. No staging area, automatic rebasing of descendants. Works on top of existing git repos. This is probably the most interesting option for your workflow since you like amending commits.
- **Graphite** — stacked PRs on GitHub, more relevant for team workflows.
- **git-town** — automates branch/sync workflows, lighter weight.

My recommendation: enforce the branch workflow from CLAUDE.md consistently, protect main on GitHub, and look into **jj** if the amend-heavy workflow appeals to you — it was designed exactly for that pattern without the force-push danger.

Want me to add a note to CLAUDE.md reinforcing "no direct commits to main" for non-trivial changes?

> DEVELOPER

I like the suggestions and I should look into jj. So for today let's do (1.) What's the releationship between (2.) and (3.)?

> AGENT

**2 and 3 are the same idea at different levels:**

- **(2) Protect main (GitHub/GitLab)** — enforced on the *hosting platform*. You configure it in the GitHub repo settings UI. It's a server-side rule that rejects force-pushes (and optionally requires PRs, status checks, reviews, etc.).

- **(3) `receive.denyNonFastForwards`** — enforced on a *bare git repo*. This is the raw git-level equivalent for self-hosted repos (e.g., a bare repo on a server you SSH into). If you're using GitHub, you'd never touch this — GitHub's branch protection is the higher-level version of it.

**For you:** Since you're on GitHub, just use (2). Go to repo Settings > Branches > Add branch protection rule for `main`, check "Restrict force pushes." That's it — (3) is irrelevant unless you host your own git server.

    95→- **`cmd` vs `cmd .` for passthroughs:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), but `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    96→- **Markdown refs:** Multiple references use `[1],[2]` not `[1,2]` or `[1][2]` (both break in markdown)
    97→
    98→## Feature Workflow
    99→
   100→**Before starting feature or fix work:**
   101→1. Create a branch: `git checkout -b <type>-<short-description>` (e.g., `fix-compare-optional-p`)
   102→2. Create a dated entry in `notes/chores-N.md` with context and plan
   103→3. Update `notes/todo.md` to move items to In Progress
   104→4. Bump version in `Cargo.toml` (always required — use `X.Y.Z-dev` for multi-step, direct bump for single-step)
   105→5. For plans: recommend single-step (one commit) vs multi-step (`-devN` series) and get user approval
   106→6. Commit the above as a chore marker commit before starting code changes
   107→7. Use todo list to track progress during implementation
   108→
   109→**On completion:**
   110→1. Update `notes/chores-N.md` with result
   111→2. Move items from In Progress to Done in `notes/todo.md`
   112→3. Remove `-dev` from version in `Cargo.toml`
   113→4. Commit as a release chore (include `Cargo.lock` if changed)
   114→
   115→**Branch naming:** `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`
   116→
   117→## Verification Workflow
   118→
   119→**After code changes, run verification and install immediately:**
   120→```bash
   121→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tspec tests to verify changes with "old" binary; if all pass:
   122→tspec test -p tspec-build      # Run tspec-build tests
   123→tspec install --path .         # Install ASAP so we dogfood the new binary
   124→tspec test -p tspec            # Run tests again with the new binary to catch issues early
   125→tspec clippy
   126→tspec fmt --check
   127→```
   128→
   129→**After committing code, remind about .claude/ files:**

   131→Committed abc123.

   138→## Git Operations Claude Cannot Perform
   139→
   140→Due to circular references with `.claude/` session files (which record Claude's behavior), Claude cannot perform these git operations:

   142→- `git checkout` - switching branches affects session files

```
     1→# tspec - Translation Spec Build System
     2→
     3→A spec-driven build system for Rust that wraps cargo with configurable target triples, compiler flags, and linker options. Each tspec applies to a Cargo **package** — the unit with a `Cargo.toml`. If different crates need different compilation settings, they should be in separate packages.
     4→
     5→**Status:** Active development. Works with both workspaces and single-package projects, Plain Old Packages (POPs).
     6→
     7→## Installation
     8→
     9→```bash
    10→cargo install --path .
    11→```
    12→
    13→Or run directly:
    14→```bash
    15→cargo run -- build
    16→```
    17→
    18→## Usage
    19→
    20→```bash
    21→tspec build                                # Build current package (or all in workspace) with tspec.ts.toml if present
    22→tspec build -p myapp                       # Build specific package with tspec.ts.toml if present
    23→tspec build myapp                          # Same, using positional argument
    24→tspec build .                              # Build the package in current directory
    25→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt          # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    26→tspec build -p myapp -t tspec-opt.ts.toml  # Build with alternative spec tspec-opt.ts.toml
    27→tspec build -p myapp -r -s                 # Build release, strip symbols with tspec.ts.toml if present
    28→tspec run -p myapp                         # Build and run with tspec.ts.toml if present
    29→tspec test -p myapp                        # Build and test with tspec.ts.toml if present
    30→tspec build -w                             # Build all packages (even from inside a package dir)
    31→tspec compare -p myapp                     # Compare binary sizes for a package
    32→tspec compare -p myapp -t *.ts.toml        # Compare using shell-expanded glob
    33→tspec compare                              # Compare all packages (workspace mode)
    34→tspec compare -w                           # Force workspace mode from inside a package
    35→tspec compare -w -f                        # Workspace mode, stop on first failure
    36→```
    37→
    38→The `-p` flag or positional argument specifies a package by name or path (defaults to current directory if in a package, otherwise all packages). Paths like `.` are resolved to the actual cargo package name. At a pure workspace root (no `[package]`), `.` means "all packages."
    39→Use `-w, --workspace` to force all-packages mode even when inside a package directory.
    40→The `-t` flag selects a tspec file; if omitted and `tspec.ts.toml` exists, it's used automatically.
    41→
    42→**Note on `cmd` vs `cmd .`:** For passthrough commands (clean, clippy, fmt), `tspec cmd` passes no `-p` to cargo (operates on everything), while `tspec cmd .` resolves to `cargo cmd -p <name>` (package-specific). The difference is most visible with `clean`: `cargo clean` removes all of target/ while `cargo clean -p <name>` leaves shared metadata files. This matches cargo's own behavior. Use `tspec clean` or `tspec clean -w` for a full clean.
    43→
    44→## tspec Files
    45→
    46→Translation specs are TOML files (conventionally `*.ts.toml`) that configure builds:
    47→
    48→```toml
    49→# tspec.ts.toml - all supported fields shown
    50→
    51→# Top-level high-level options (expand to lower-level cargo/rustc flags)
    52→panic = "abort"                            # "unwind" (default), "abort", "immediate-abort" (nightly)
    53→strip = "symbols"                          # "none" (default), "debuginfo", "symbols"
    54→rustflags = ["-C", "relocation-model=static"]  # raw flags passed through to RUSTFLAGS
    55→
```

User merges branch to main (Claude cannot do checkouts/merges)\n\n**Branch naming:** `<type>-<description>` where type is `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `chore`\n\n**Force-push safety:** Force-pushing is acceptable on feature branches (use `--force-with-lease` when possible). Never force-push to main — main should have branch protection enabled on GitHub."
}

> AGENT

Here's the conflict:

- Top-level `rustflags` → collected into `rustc_flags` vec → set as `RUSTFLAGS` env var
- `[cargo.config.build] rustflags = [...]` → passed as `--config build.rustflags=[...]`

Cargo's precedence rules: **`RUSTFLAGS` env var takes precedence over `--config`** (env > CLI config > file config). So if both are set, `cargo.config.build.rustflags` would be silently ignored.

That's a confusing trap. The `build.rustflags` example in the README is misleading — it suggests a path that won't work if the user also has top-level `rustflags` (or `panic`/`strip`, which also feed into RUSTFLAGS).

Actually, it's worse: even with an *empty* top-level `rustflags = []`, if `panic` or `strip` are set, those generate RUSTFLAGS entries, which means `RUSTFLAGS` is set, which means `--config build.rustflags` is ignored.

I should remove `build.rustflags` from the example and note the conflict. Want me to replace that example with something that doesn't overlap, like `[cargo.config.target.*.runner]` or `[cargo.config.env]`?

> DEVELOPER

yes, replace it with something that doesn't overlap, and actually what would `[cargo.config.env]` actually set "env" where?

> AGENT

`[env]` in cargo config sets environment variables for all build processes — rustc invocations, build scripts, proc macros. So `--config env.MY_VAR="value"` would make `MY_VAR` available during compilation. It's documented in [cargo's config reference](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html#env) but it's a bit niche for an example.

Let me replace with something more practical that doesn't overlap.

   115→affects ALL compilations (including dependency build scripts), causing crashes
   116→with flags like `-nostartfiles`. The generated build.rs scopes flags to just

   118→
   119→### Cargo Config Passthrough (`cargo.config`)
   120→
   121→The `[cargo.config]` section is a general-purpose passthrough to cargo's `--config KEY=VALUE` mechanism. Any nested tables under `[cargo.config]` are flattened into dotted keys and passed as `--config` args. This lets you override any [cargo configuration](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/config.html) value at the command line.
   122→
   123→**Profile settings** are the most common use case:
   124→
   125→```toml
   126→[cargo.config.profile.release]
   127→opt-level = "z"
   128→codegen-units = 1
   129→lto = true
   130→```
   131→
   132→This produces: `cargo --config profile.release.opt-level="z" --config profile.release.codegen-units=1 --config profile.release.lto=true`
   133→
   134→**Other cargo config keys** work too:
   135→
   136→```toml
   137→[cargo.config.build]
   138→rustflags = ["-C", "target-cpu=native"]
   139→

That's the right approach. Here's the sequence:

1. I commit the current code changes (excluding `.claude/`)
2. You `/exit`
3. You do the git surgery:
   ```bash
   git checkout -b refactor-config-rename    # branch from current HEAD
   git checkout main
   git reset --hard 4af2270                  # back to v0.11.5
   git push --force-with-lease               # fix main on remote
   git checkout refactor-config-rename
   git push -u origin refactor-config-rename
   ```
4. Restart Claude on the new branch

The `.claude/` files will record being on `main` in the old session, but that's fine — the new session will pick up the correct branch. The transcript history is just context, not state that needs to match the branch.
